dated after the 10-23 05:08 PDT owner reset. Test merges were made in ~/wt/root-merge (detached), then main was

NOTE: localhost has no system pytest/matplotlib, and the ubuntu shell is not in the docker group yet. I used a venv
(scratchpad rm/venv, --system-site-packages + pytest + matplotlib) and ran the tests under `sg docker -c` (the bind
tests need docker yosys). Without docker, 23 bind tests fail on the environment, not on the code.

## QC section (ROOT-MERGE resumed 2026-10-24 07:20 PDT after the coordinator restart)
- The killed agent left ~/wt/root-merge mid-merge of qc-p6-a0v6. Its die_plan.json was rewritten at 07:03:22, the minute it was killed, so that run may not have finished. I re-ran `physical/die_plan/qwen_compute/run.sh` cleanly in two trees:
  - origin/main 8a5d1f3dab alone: die_plan.json comes out byte-identical to the committed file. G0 PASS, 0 errors, 88 warnings, G0C 52/56.
  - main merged with a0v6 (gen_plan.py +3 lines that call a0_core_taps.declare): G0 FAIL, 404 errors, all check=clock, "clock tap ... outside the outline". By type: sle_su_c 82, slw_su_c 82, and 60 each for sle_su_l0, sle_su_l1, slw_su_l0, slw_su_l1. Report: ~/coord/evidence/root-merge/qc-a0v6-on-main_g0_report.json
- Decision: HOLD. I aborted the merge (`git merge --abort`); ~/wt/root-merge is clean at df641549db. a0v6 declares its taps against the pre-g0c QC plan (19:28). Main's plan was rebuilt later (qc-p6-g0c 22:59, SU split into hard cores, grid taps by default).
- The kits and tools (physical/ref_tile/qfd_tile_n*_a0c*, tools/qwen_romleaf/*) are new files only and conflict with nothing. They travel with lane QC-A0 (claude/qc-a0-1024, merge a34546df17), which owns the A0 re-kit.
- WARNING for QC-A0: a34546df17 took the a0v6 gen_plan.py hook and the plan_notes a0_core_taps hunk, but kept main's die_plan.json without regenerating it. The committed plan therefore does not match its generator, and regenerating it gives G0 FAIL as shown above. Before asking to merge: fix the a0_core_taps declaration for the current plan geometry, or drop the hook, then run run.sh and get G0 PASS.
- Merge requests: lanes append `MERGE REQUEST: <branch> @sha <claim>` below. ROOT-MERGE checks this queue every ~10 min.

2026-10-25 04:44 PDT GEOM-REVIEW-2: CROSS-CUTTING (routed reviews, 25 runs so far on S81/QC/KV/HBM): the single largest common cause of FF-hold and face-setup misses is the IO clock basis -- vclk source latency fixed at 250 (or the TT insertion) at BOTH TT and FF while routed face/pin-reg clocks are 177-229 at FF (HBM n12/argmax, ve_s, rxsf, hd_st_m, KV io_*) or 330-690 (vn_a/vn_b, attn_hcore, pv_pair). Required methodology fix (owner decision 5e / METHODOLOGY 9.7, HBM-CORES ref CORE_FF_VCLK 32e1c492c): per-corner virtual-clock latency = the routed RCX pin-reg/tap mean of THAT corner (and for ft partitions the neighbour station's tap), set by the kit generator + sdc_lint, signoff overlay keeps it; plus taps actually landed on the planned insertion (+-20). Until it lands, every rebuild will re-hit the same output/input hold walls. Second common cause: ft stations planned 2 per 1-2 mm slot (KV io_*/hroot, QC coll_ftc) and ft nets on M2/M3.

[COORD 2026-10-25 05:40 PDT] S81-CORES RULINGS (technical, coordinator decides):
- (1) Split-core insertion (~360 ps vs 250+-40): accepted if bound per corner (decision 5e / METHODOLOGY 9.7). The routed pin-register I is recorded per corner in the core's views, and the parent/die budget takes it with an early tap. Industry practice is to annotate block latency and let the parent balance it. A core does not need to hit 250 by itself; skew inside the core must still meet the 20 ps balance. This needs FLOW's per-corner binding (READY ae2710f26).
- (2) eng_rr_e GRT 235k: re-floorplan the macros, structurally. Put them on the edges/abutted, with horizontal channels for M2/M4 pin access and routing between macro rows, then G2 before kit. M6 grant only as a fallback if the G2 of the re-floorplan still overflows.
- (3) qbank_S and xfin outlines grow in the S81-PLAN2 batched delta, derived top-down from the parent slot. xfin needs >=72.5k um2 cells / ~0.6 fill, about 120k um2. Render it before the kit.
- ot_s81eng_aq_n32 exponent inputs tied to 0 (LEC finding) violate the no-tie-off rule: fix the RTL before any eng core rebuild (S81-CORES2).

PROPOSAL IO-HOLD [CLOCK-RESET 2026-10-25 06:54 PDT] (analysis only, no gate change made): contract tol 40 -> 30 ps (kit set_input_delay -min -30; die_sta stand-in OT_BHOLD_30p0) AND IO paths carry no HOLD_SLACK_MARGIN (keep 15 on reg->reg). Signed kits stay valid (both only loosen).
(1) Seam safety, from die STA FF with the 40 ps cell (intel2 clock-reset/hold run_bhc_FF): worst seam hold +31.09 (head, pair skew 5.30), S81 +32.85 (3.53), KV +33.79 (2.81) -> tol-independent term B = slack - tol + skew = -3.6 ps (clk->q - t_hold - 25 unc + wire; sender unc/2 and both kits' 15 ps margin NOT credited). Seam slack = tol + B - skew - eRCX. tol 30 at the 20 ps related-skew bound: +6.4 ps left for routed-RCX latency error (measured pair skew today 2.5-5.3 -> +21). tol 25 leaves +1.4 = too thin. Dropping the IO margin does not change this sum (die STA never credited it).
(2) Hold buffers (census tool: hold* chains walked through input*/output* port buffers from each port). Signed kits: hrow_c 17.9k hold bufs = 10.6k input-side (5.5/input bit) + 3.3k output + 4.0k internal; vq_lfp 8.7k = 3.0k in / 0.3k out / 5.4k internal; sstn_payload536 2.8k = 1.2k / 0.5k / 1.1k; cfg7_seq_bfp_c 2966 = 2436 in (4.7/bit) / 228 out / 302 internal -> input side dominates (35-80%). Measured what-if, cfg7 CTS rerun from its 3_place (diagnostic, deleted): tol40/m15 2966 (hold area 25.6% of cells) | tol30/m15 2605 (-12%) | tol25/m15 2507 (-15%) | tol30 + margin 0 1778 (-40%, hold area 17.1%). So the tol cut alone is ~12%; the double-counted 15 ps IO margin (per side, plus 12.5 unc per side, on top of the die-STA 25 unc) is the bigger lever. Extrapolated to the 4 kits: ~-30..40% IO hold bufs (hrow ~-4.5k).
(3) Sender side: no gain. A seam bit is point-to-point, so the same ps must be added once on the path; the sender already uses its clk->q credit (outputs need only 1-2 bufs/bit today). Keep the tol at the receiver (it times its real per-flop capture I); do not split (two roundings + two repair overshoots).
Also seen: legacy kits (gen_core_kit_legacy) use fixed vclk 250 + CORNERS tc bc, so TT (I_TT ~256 > 250) drives input padding although hold is signed at FF -- F1 per-corner I removes that. Implementation if approved: gen_phys hold_in_min_ps 30, die_sta default bhold 30, IO margin via set_clock_uncertainty (+15 hold on clk->clk only) instead of global HOLD_SLACK_MARGIN. OWNER? none (contract number inside the owner TT/FF rule).

[COORD 2026-10-25 07:01 PDT] OWNER DECISION (strap question): option B. ID straps (tile/lane/head IDs such as HBM tile_tid, map_lane_j, lane_lane_id and KV head_eighth BSEL/HID, astk variant IDs) become INPUT PINS of ONE hardened design, tied to constants at the parent.
Allowed for ID straps ONLY: every other input stays driven, the no-tie-off rule is unchanged, and R7 is amended for IDs alone.
Implementation rules:
(1) Strap ports are declared in the plan core/type entry as "straps": [...], named strap_*. The port-bind / tie-off audit accepts ties only on declared straps.
(2) Inside the block they are quasi-static: no pin register needed; the kit SDC sets set_false_path -from [strap ports]; the hardened block must work for ALL values (no case analysis).
(3) At the die, the parent ties them with TIEHI/TIELO cells next to the pin.
(4) LEC/lockstep: each master vs every original constant variant under the matching strap values. Token tests use at least two different IDs per master.
Owners: HBM lane (HBM cores 226 -> ~71), KV lane (head_eighth 8 -> 3, astk 4 -> 1), FLOW-FIX (audit whitelist + SDC + METHODOLOGY R7 amendment), owners-distinct (one item per master).

[COORD 2026-10-25 08:06 PDT] RULING on the HBM analog clock-input escape (Codex OWNER? lines 1251/1436). This is a technical call, so it is decided here and not sent to the owner. The PHY is a modelled macro (owner 10-14: only PHY/off-chip signals are modelled), so we own its abstract.
Contract:
(1) Each pdie_serdes u_lk_* macro declares ONE digital clock-input pin, CK_DIG, on the macro face toward the core, on M4/M5. Its coordinates are written into the macro LEF and the plan entry. That pin is the only clock sink of the macro.
(2) A dedicated digital escape strip of at least 8.64 um sits outside the analog PG halo, at that pin. It holds one driver buffer, BUFx4/x6, plus the tap. The die clock tree ends at that buffer. The buffer-to-pin wire is <=20 um.
(3) The analog macros never move. Taps sit on the escape strip, not at the generic budget sites. tap_clear must not relocate analog taps.
(4) STA: the insertion at CK_DIG is the PHY reference. Inside the macro, the PHY clock is modelled as ideal, with a fixed declared latency in the macro .lib.
Owner: HBM plan (B2o). This item is NOT on the selected HBM one-path, so it is lower priority than PATH-HBM work.

Contract = the minimum safe seam split: sender kit set_output_delay -min -sender_ps, receiver set_input_delay -min (sender_ps - seam_ps), seam_ps >= 25 (= die tap related-skew bound 20 + RCX 5; the die 25 ps hold unc is already the two 12.5 kit halves). Derivation: sender out-hold gives port >= vclk+12.5+sender_ps, receiver in-hold gives port->D >= C-vclk+t_hold+12.5-in_min; summed in the die frame seam slack >= seam_ps - (e_rx - e_tx). Today -40/0 credits no clk->q although every partition output is a pin register.
Evidence results/clock_iohold_0810 (FF, kit 4_cts, RCX-fit RC): (1) payload536 (signed) registered outputs cq+port = 55.5..58 ps, launch spread 187-195 -> sender_ps 25 costs 0 output pads. PROPOSAL IO-HOLD-2 [KITS-SPARE/LB 2026-10-25 08:30 PDT] (OWNER?/COORD; analysis only, no kit built) S81 cfg7bfp c2 (epyc1:/srv/opentallas-scratch2/claude/s81-cores3-lb/kits/ot_s81_cfg7_seq_bfp_c2, 4_cts.odb, FF libs, vclk FF 161.6, setRC_rcx placement RC; probe epyc1:/srv/opentallas-scratch2/claude/kits-spare/lb/cfg7probe/{probe.tcl,probe_ff.tsv,probe_tt.tsv,out_*.tsv}). Builds on PROPOSAL IO-HOLD 06:54.
 (1) The per-core input min -40 is pessimistic by ~90 ps against a real same-generator neighbour. All 519 input pin regs fail FF hold at -47.5..-50.8 (sum 26.1 ns): arrival = vclk 161.6 - 40 + input buf 14 = 135.8 vs capture CK 160.6 + 12.5 seam unc + 12.4 lib hold = 185.5. The same kit's own output side (the launch a neighbour sees): pin reg CK 155.3 + clk->q 38.5 (DFFASRHQNx1 FF) + INV/BUF 22 = port arrival 216.5, i.e. +52..+55 ps after the FF bound. All 114 outputs meet out-min 0 + 12.5 by +39..+48. So the contract checks the sender at 'arrival >= I+12.5' but lets the receiver assume 'arrival >= I-40': the 52.5-ps gap is the hold buffer wall on every S81 core kit (cfg7 c2 924 bufs + RSZ-0060; hcore_eh1 6067 input endpoints; vmr_s6h 4054 input bufs; head elem -57/input). A consistent industry contract: receiver in_min = sender's published out-min guarantee (0 + its 12.5 seam half), and the pair skew (neighbour tap vs this tap) checked once at the parent as a related-pair hold check with CPPR (decision 5), not as a -tol in every core. With in_min = +12.5 the c2 input slack is about +2.6, so ~0 input hold buffers. Even 'in_min = 0 - 25 pair skew' leaves about -12/input (vs -50 now).
 (2) Kit-level alternative evaluated, a dedicated early input-landing tap (all 519 input pin regs on one regional tap at I - delta): input hold needs delta >= 51 (55 to clear all). At delta 55 the TT input setup room is 495 ps (TT input setup slack 550..559 today), but 241 pin-reg -> successor FF holds then fail (p50 -12, worst -47.7, sum 3.5 ns, i.e. 87% less padding than today). It does NOT fit the per-corner contract: the input pin regs would sit 55 ps (> tol 40, > 20 window) off the bound pin-reg insertion, which the post-CTS/sign-off insertion gate FAILs. It is a contract change too (a published per-core input-landing latency I_in = I - 55, parent seam setup pays 55 of ~550 TT room). Not built.
 Ask OWNER/COORD: pick (A) in_min = sender out-min guarantee (+12.5 per seam half), with the parent related-pair hold check (CPPR) carrying the pair skew [recommended: removes the input wall on all S81/QC/KV/HBM cores, no area]; or (B) publish an input-landing latency per core (I_in = I - 55, early regional tap; keeps ~1/8 of the padding, moves it inside); or (C) keep -40 and accept ~1 hold buffer chain per input pin reg (+25..35% area on small cores). Until then cfg7bfp (row 1ea6b2850107) stays not routed.

READY [FLOW-CANARY 2026-10-25 11:34 PDT] claude/canary-bind-1025@15dd0b1a2 (base main 5236ba8c1; tools/autopilot/canary_gate.py + test): the autopilot binding rule was wrong in two ways. (a) A corrective row had to equal the NEWEST canary string exactly, so each trigger launch (every flow-tool merge) staled it. 51bb9753cb85 and 31a67854973e hold on 'corrective reference must equal newest canary 183f...', and 31a6's string is branch@sha@path. (b) A newer PENDING canary masked a completed PASS for ~1.5-2 h after every main tool merge. New rule: bind to the newest COMPLETED canary of the reference die (canary.json src == meta src_commit). On a PASS the kit's watched flow must equal THAT canary's flow, and main must equal it too unless a newer pending canary already covers main's flow. A corrective is identified by the path after the last '@'; it is allowed while that canary FAILed and no newer completed PASS exists, or when it names the newest pending run. No gate is lowered: a FAIL still holds every normal row. Tests: test_canary_gate 17 PASS, tools/autopilot 81 PASS/1 skip. Replay on the live state (11:35): 31a67854973e becomes a CORRECTIVE submit and the rest stay held on real FAILs.

PROPOSAL [CLOCK-RESET 2026-10-25 14:32 PDT] RULE LC1 (late-capture class: KV kv3_io_ft, S81 slice t12/t41/t42, QC coll_ftm + 21-tap START/rst, CP1 col_h10 r0/r1, head IOA_r2): ONE structural rule. "Every boundary pin register (data ft/bus stations AND control inputs START/run/rst/cfg) is captured within [L_c - 20, L_c + 20] ps at EACH sign-off corner c in {TT, FF}, where L_c is the partition's declared interface latency for that corner (vclk TT / its own FF value from the per-corner contract, never TT x ratio), measured per pin register, not as a mean." Structure that delivers it: (a) taps: the pin regs of a face are their own density tap groups (bounded sinks ~<=500, bounded span along the face ~<=150 um, the face's control flops included), never a whole station band / interior tree on one tap, no sink-less grid taps; (b) latency: L comes from the parent's balanced trunk / declared core latency (die_clock core-early contract), leaf trees <=2 levels; series pad only <=20 ps per leaf group, never at a root shared with interior sinks (CP1 had 427-514 BUFx4 root pads to lift 80-178 ps trees to 250); (c) gate: postcts_gate reads every pin reg on GRT RC at TT and FF -> FAIL_POSTCTS STRUCTURAL out of window (re-tap), not hold repair; sign-off reports the same window on SPEF; (d) contract: with capture <= L_c+20, IO-HOLD option A (sender out-min +12.5 + parent CPPR pair check) replaces the -40 input min, so input hold needs ~16-36 ps of data delay, not 5-deep BUFx2 chains. Evidence: the mean gate PASSes these kits while the TAIL fails hold: CP1 r1 signoff SPEF pin flops TT 229-304 / FF 174-248 (spread 74; FF vclk rebound to the 195.5 mean -> tail +52 late -> hold -30.85/1626), GRT pre-route same kit TT 229-265 / FF 177-210 (window probe intel2:/srv/opentallas-scratch2/claude/clock-reset/lc1probe/r1): the +39 ps tail appears only after detail route (CP1 sub-lane finding which leaves). KV io_ft/t12 capture 285/460 FF vs 202.5 (group taps); t41/t42 285.6 FF (11 BUFx24 levels on a 1-D band); QC START/rst input -167.66 (control flops outside density groups). Gate code READY below (report-only); enforcement OT_POSTCTS_WINDOW=1 + per-face tap groups go default-on in ONE batched change after the 5 canaries show window PASS -> COORD rule? (technical, within decision 5; no owner question).

It gates on the reference partition's result vs its recorded BASELINE (same partition, last canary-passed main, or the reference run). Each metric (insertion, TT setup WNS, FF hold WNS, GRT overflow, DRV) may be no worse than baseline by more than its tolerance (insertion 5 ps, WNS 10 ps, hold endpoints +5%). It does NOT gate on an absolute PASS of the reference design. Absolute failures of a reference (s81 t12 / KV io_ft insertion 226-229 vs 250, QC coll_ftm FF hold -136, HBM TKM taps 522) are DESIGN items owned by their lanes and stop blocking every route. A new reference with no baseline runs once to set it (INFO, non-gating). The KV core canary (head_eighth_e1 TT -222.85 vs ref +36.61, async reset recovery) IS a regression -> it gates KV core rows until CLOCK-RESET/FLOW fix it.
